1. ราคา ≤ $34 (เข้า re-entry watch zone $24–34)
2. Circulation (USDC in circulation) โต +QoQ 2 ไตรมาสติดต่อกัน + market share ขึ้นเหนือ 26–27%

4. Fed terminal rate ≥ 3.25% (float-income headwind คลายตัว — rate ไม่ลงลึกอย่างที่ตลาด price)

**บทเรียนสำคัญของ batch นี้:**
1. Batch ใช้เวลา 12 วันกว่าจะปิดครบ (commission 09-01 → ปิด 09-12/13) — เป็น argument ให้พิจารณา harder SLA สำหรับ Re-Analysis Trigger Rule แทน soft priority queue
2. TGR ceiling non-compliance (>3.0%) เกิดซ้ำ 2 ครั้งใน 3 สัปดาห์ (GOOGL แล้ว ADSK) — ทั้งคู่จับได้ก่อนส่ง Morgan formal ไม่ใช่ทีหลัง — ควรเป็น checklist ข้อแรกที่ Emma/Quinn เช็คเองตั้งแต่เริ่ม DCF ไม่ใช่รอ QA/Charlie จับทีหลัง (ประหยัด re-compute cascade)
3. Data-integrity errors 2 จุดถูกจับและแก้ระหว่าง batch (CRM analyst-downgrade-wave ผิด, GOOGL Cloud-backlog $155B ผิดจริง $514B) — ยืนยันคุณค่าของ full-pipeline cross-verify เทียบ news.js เดิม
4. Session Interruption/Resume Protocol ถูกใช้งานหนักมาก (10+ instances) จาก API session limit ที่เกิดถี่ขึ้นเรื่อยๆ ตลอด batch — Step 0 (เช็คไฟล์ dated ก่อนเริ่มทุกครั้ง) ป้องกัน rework/entry ซ้อนได้ผลจริงทุกครั้ง

**Rationale (all 5):** Regime RISK-ON, Force Deploy ACTIVE, cash ~74% of NAV -- a full-pipeline-cleared candidate would have been live-eligible for a real BUY this round. This round broke the pattern of the prior five sector-rotation rounds (Energy/Industrials/Healthcare/Financials/Real-Estate, all showing very few Filter-A survivors): **3 of 5 candidates cleared Filter A** (RCL -31.9%, CCL -35.8%, BKNG -27.1%), and one (BKNG) reached the Learning-queue lane -- the closest any candidate has come to fast-track since Round 42's AXP (which died one filter later, at D). As the task brief anticipated, the sub-sector was a genuine mixed bag, not uniformly beaten-down or uniformly cheap: hotel/lodging pure-plays (MAR, HLT) sit well within 20% of 52-week highs (near-ATH, resilient RevPAR/demand trends), scanned honestly and correctly rejected rather than force-fit, while cruise lines (RCL, CCL) took real, verified hits from fuel-cost/oil-price spikes (Brent crude spike on Middle East/Strait of Hormuz tensions) and BKNG took a hit from AI-disintermediation-fear sentiment (Meta Muse launch) plus a resolved EU antitrust loss (ETraveli acquisition blocked). All three survivors classified cyclical/sentiment (temporary), not permanent impairment, given genuinely strong or accelerating underlying operating results (CCL: 12th consecutive record-net-yield quarter; RCL: adjusted EPS +33% YoY, Perfecta Program targets 20% EPS CAGR + ROIC ≥17%; BKNG: Q1 2026 revenue +16% YoY, GAAP net income +225% YoY). All three classified Value bucket (revenue growth below the 15% B-V1 trigger in each case, including BKNG's borderline 12.85% TTM read) -- no B-V1/D-V1 Growth-lane peer-selection exercise needed this round. RCL died at Filter B on a capex-depressed negative FCF yield despite the 32% pullback (EV/EBITDA also sits above, not below, its own median -- the "beaten down from an elevated peak, still not statistically cheap" pattern). CCL died at Filter C on a conservative call: ROIC 65.3% of WACC falls short of the 80% bar, and while CCL's TTM ROIC (7.76%) is meaningfully above its single-quarter May print (5.71%) -- suggestive of the kind of improving trend CLAUDE.md's exception clause is designed for -- a clean, fully-sourced quarterly ROIC time series confirming ≥2 consecutive quarters of increase could not be assembled within this round's capped search budget (per the task's explicit instruction to cap searches and mark genuinely-hard-to-find data as inconclusive rather than loop), so the conservative REJECT was applied and CCL flagged for quick re-verification in a future round. BKNG's B-Zero red flag (negative equity from a debt/earnings-funded buyback program, not operating losses) was handled per the established leveraged-recap carve-out precedent (BBWI) with full transparency. BKNG's Filter D/E lane-routing to Learning-queue (not fast-track) is a deliberately conservative read -- the +45.6% Street PT gap could have argued for a looser interpretation, but the mandatory D-Legacy naive-FV method (0.860× price/FV) governs per the letter of the rule, and the unresolved AI-disintermediation overhang correctly fails Filter E's third sub-criterion rather than being waved through. **Max Consultation Rule note:** no candidate reached Fast-track/Full Pipeline this round (BKNG reached Learning-queue only, one lane below Fast-track), so the mandatory Charlie pre-trade consultation was never triggered -- nothing to simulate/log beyond this rationale entry. No Full Pipeline run, no `reports/[TICKER]_2026-09-29.md` written for any of the 5, no trade executed, no `dashboard/portfolio.js` or `trade-log.md`/`positions.md` changes. CCL logged to `portfolio/buy_list.md` as the round's furthest-through-funnel near-miss-reject with a ROIC-trend re-verification watch trigger. BKNG journaled to `agent_notes/charlie/learning_queue_2026-09.md` per Tier 1.5 protocol with specific Emma/Bear-lite open questions flagged (full DCF vs. naive-FV reconciliation identified as highest-value next step). **Sector-level flag for CIO/Charlie:** this round breaks the "broad market strength, few beaten-down candidates" pattern of the prior five sector-rotation rounds -- cruise/travel/lodging in 2026 is a genuinely differentiated sub-sector with real, identifiable fuel-cost and AI-sentiment-driven dislocations layered on top of otherwise-strong fundamentals, distinct from the uniform near-ATH pattern seen in Energy/Industrials/Healthcare/Financials/Real-Estate. **Rationale (all 5):** Regime RISK-ON, Force Deploy ACTIVE, cash ~74% of NAV -- a full-pipeline-cleared candidate would have been live-eligible for a real BUY this round. Materials as a GICS sector label masked sharply divergent 2026 commodity-cycle realities across sub-industries: copper (FCX), steel (NUE), industrial gases (LIN), and ag-chem/seed (CTVA) all sit within 6-15% of their respective 52-week highs -- genuine, verified resilience (AI-datacenter/grid copper demand, tariff-protected steel pricing, structurally defensive industrial-gas demand, moderate ag-chem softness only) -- correctly scanned honestly and rejected at Filter A rather than force-fit, per the task's explicit "real candidates if there's been a real commodity price down-cycle" framing (there wasn't one for these four). **MOS (fertilizer/potash) was the exception**: a genuine, severe commodity price down-cycle (Q2 2026 net loss $272.8M vs +$410.7M net income prior year, gross margin collapse $518.6M→$214.7M YoY, FY2026 phosphate production guidance withdrawn, capex cut as a rational capital-discipline response) drove the deepest pullback of any Materials name scanned (-36.1% to -38.2%, among the deepest of any sector-rotation round to date) -- correctly classified as a legitimate cyclical/commodity-price dislocation (not permanent impairment: no plant closures/exits, no market-share loss, no accounting irregularity cited) and passed through to B-Zero (equity $11.464B solidly positive, no negative-equity red flag) and Filter B. MOS died at Filter B on a decisive 2-of-3-leg value-trap pattern: FCF yield -12.95% (deeply negative, net-loss-driven) and EV/EBITDA 12.00x sitting 63% *above*, not below, its own 10-year median of 7.36x -- the same "price fell but earnings fell faster" pattern seen with F/LEN in Round 44a, confirming that a real, verified commodity down-cycle does not automatically imply statistical cheapness if the multiple itself expanded as the earnings denominator collapsed faster than the price. MOS's elevated-vs-own-history leverage (Debt/Equity 27% above 10Y median, thin 1.83x interest coverage) was correctly carried into the Filter E-relevant financial-flexibility discussion rather than treated as a B-Zero hard-reject (Debt/EBITDA 3.06x sits below the >4-5x threshold). Filter C/D/E were not fully computed for MOS once Filter B failed decisively on 2 legs -- the available ROIC data point (2.78% TTM vs ~9% WACC ≈ 31% of WACC) was checked as a zero-additional-cost directional cross-check and confirms rather than contradicts the reject, a tight-budget judgment call consistent with the task's explicit instruction to avoid the prior 50x-search-loop failure mode. **Max Consultation Rule note:** no candidate reached Fast-track/Full Pipeline this round (MOS died at Filter B, well short of Tier 1.5 lane assignment), so the mandatory Charlie pre-trade consultation was never triggered -- nothing to simulate/log beyond this rationale entry. No Full Pipeline run, no `reports/[TICKER]_2026-09-29.md` written for any of the 5, no trade executed, no `dashboard/portfolio.js` or `trade-log.md`/`positions.md` changes. MOS logged to `portfolio/buy_list.md` as the round's sole Filter-A survivor and near-miss with a potash/phosphate price-cycle-turn watch trigger. **Sector-level flag for CIO/Charlie:** this is the fourth consecutive sector-rotation round (after Consumer Discretionary autos/homebuilders R44a and cruise/travel/lodging R44b) to surface at least one real, identifiable cyclical dislocation that nonetheless fails Filter B on fundamentals -- confirming the VALUE-FIRST screen continues to correctly distinguish "genuinely cheap" from "beaten-down because earnings/EBITDA collapsed faster than price," even within a sector (commodities) explicitly expected by the task brief to contain real cyclical candidates. Counter-factual: N/A (no position taken). See `portfolio/watchlist.md` § Scout Report Round 45 for full detail.

This is the second dedicated Industrials-sector scout round, deliberately diversifying from Round 40's Machinery/Electrical/Diversified-Industrial/Transports focus (BA/CAT/GE/HON/DE/ETN/EMR/PH/FDX/UPS) into defense primes and aerospace specifically (LMT/RTX/NOC/GD/TXT), per the task's explicit instruction to check honestly rather than assume any name passes given defense primes' strong multi-year geopolitical/defense-spending-driven re-rating. **RTX (-17.1 to -17.3%) and GD (-10.7 to -15.8%) both confirm this warning directly -- real, documented 2026 pullbacks but both short of the 20% Filter A floor**, remaining relatively well-supported by their own recent execution (Collins/Pratt aftermarket for RTX, Gulfstream/Electric Boat backlog for GD). **LMT, NOC, and TXT (3/5) PASS Filter A decisively** (-24.9 to -25.1%, -32.4 to -34.7%, -24.9% respectively), each independently verified as cyclical/sentiment not permanent impairment: LMT's F-16/Aeronautics Q1 stumble already showed a 2-quarter improving trend culminating in a Q2 2026 beat-and-raise with record $230.4B backlog; NOC's deepest pullback of the round (-32 to -35%) tracks a broad defense-sector-wide budget/appropriations-politics selloff (not NOC-specific) on a name that simultaneously raised guidance and posted record backlog; TXT's Q2 2026 miss came with a genuine, quantified, disclosed government-funding-uncertainty risk (CR/appropriations-timing, not demand loss). All 3 passed B-Zero clean. **Filter B was the binding constraint for 2 of 3**: NOC failed decisively (0/2 legs, FCF yield well below 6%, EV/EBITDA merely near- not bottom-third of its own median); TXT failed narrowly on both legs, flagged as this round's closest Filter B near-miss (FCF yield source-dependent, EV/EBITDA a real-but-insufficient discount to median). **LMT alone reached Filter C (PASS decisively, ROIC 27.4-30.3% with an improving trend, ~3.4-3.8x an 8% mega-cap WACC) and Filter D, where it died on the strict no-growth-perpetuity naive-FV proxy** -- LMT's FCF yield (7.30%) sits modestly below the assumed 8% WACC, mechanically putting naive FV ~9% below price; the Street-PT override check (consensus +22.83-23% upside) fell just ~2pp short of CLAUDE.md's 25% bullish-divergence threshold, making this the round's single most genuine "one input away from a fast-track" judgment call. **0/5 pass full A-E -- no Tier 1/1.5, no Full Pipeline, no trade this round.** LMT logged to `buy_list.md` as the round's primary near-miss (4/5 gates cleared); TXT logged as a secondary near-miss (closest Filter B leg). This round is a useful counter-example/confirmation pair to the task's own framing: unlike the semicap round (37/38/47) where an entire sub-sector had structurally re-rated upward such that even deep pullbacks failed to reach cheap multiples, here the defense-primes group shows a genuine split -- some names (RTX, GD) still trading too close to highs to screen at all, and the ones that do pass Filter A (LMT, NOC, TXT) are beaten-down enough but not yet statistically cheap enough on Filter B/D, a distinct "real dislocation, not yet a real bargain" pattern.

Task-specified PARA substituted for CHTR after verifying PARA no longer trades (Paramount Global completed its merger with Skydance Media 2026-08-07, surviving entity trades as PSKY, which is separately the acquirer in the active WBD tender offer -- using PSKY would double-count that M&A story). This validates putting the reason-classification gate ahead of the B/C/D statistical screens: had it been skipped or softened, both names would very plausibly have sailed through Filter B (FCF yield, EV/EBITDA) and manufactured a false-positive "deep value" fast-track on two structurally-declining businesses -- the same underlying trap (cheapness masking real deterioration) as the BBWI B-Zero precedent, mirrored here on the earnings-quality side rather than the balance-sheet side. **0/5 pass full A-E -- no Tier 1/1.5, no Full Pipeline, no trade this round.** CMCSA logged to `buy_list.md` as a value-trap watch item (not a genuine near-miss) with a re-classification trigger tied to broadband net-add stabilization or Versant cable-networks spinoff completion. **Max Consultation Rule note:** no candidate reached Fast-track/Full Pipeline this round, so the mandatory Charlie pre-trade consultation was never triggered -- nothing to simulate/log beyond this rationale entry. **Sector-level flag for CIO/Charlie:** distinct new failure mode vs. all prior sector-rotation rounds -- legacy cable/media shows real, extreme, statistically-cheap-looking pullbacks that are nonetheless correctly rejected at the qualitative Filter A gate rather than a later quantitative one; future Communication Services re-scans should target non-legacy-cable sub-industries (streaming-native, digital advertising, gaming) rather than re-testing cable/DBS names. Counter-factual: N/A (no position taken, no candidate cleared). See `portfolio/watchlist.md` § Scout Report § Round 46 for full detail.

Not a clean CMCSA/CHTR-style confirmed-structural-impairment reject (Filter A itself was genuinely ambiguous, Scores revenue still accelerating +41% YoY) -- what actually disqualifies it is Filter D's decisive, sensitivity-robust valuation-gap fail: even generously haircut for regulatory risk, price still implies confidence in continued Scores-segment strength the team cannot underwrite given the unresolved Senate investigation and early-stage two-sided VantageScore adoption data. Logged to watchlist.md only (outright reject, Filter D missed by 3.5-5x not a marginal 0.80-0.90x band, so not a buy_list.md near-miss) with full both-sides reasoning + re-scout triggers (Scores YoY growth deceleration below ~15%, naive-FV gap closing to within 20% via growth or price correction, or Senate investigation resolving) |

**Rationale:** CIO directed a single-ticker scout on FICO after seeing the ~-57% drawdown headline and flagging it as a likely Filter A "permanent earnings-power impairment" trap (same pattern as Round 46's CMCSA/CHTR cord-cutting reject). Rigorous, honest re-verification found the beaten-down-*reason* is genuinely more ambiguous than the CMCSA/CHTR precedent -- unlike those names (where management had already confirmed 10+ consecutive quarters of subscriber losses, i.e. impairment already visible in the numbers), FICO's Scores segment revenue is still *accelerating* (+41% YoY) in the most recent reported quarter even after the regulatory shock, the FHFA mechanism is confirmed to be lender-optional rather than a hard mandate displacing FICO, and an independent (non-FICO) Milliman study still favors FICO's predictive accuracy over VantageScore. Had Filter A governed alone, this would correctly default to learning-queue per CLAUDE.md's own uncertainty-handling instruction ("ถ้า Max ไม่แน่ใจว่าจัดกลุ่มไหน... default เป็น learning-queue lane"), not an automatic reject. It was instead disqualified independently and decisively at **Filter D** -- even applying a substantial 15-40% haircut to normalized FCF to account for the regulatory risk (explicitly not using circular pre-shock TTM FCF as instructed), the naive FV lands at $165-240/share vs. a $840.89 price, a 3.5-5x gap that no reasonable sensitivity range closes. This demonstrates Filter D's independent gatekeeping value distinct from Filter A: a name can have a genuinely uncertain (not confirmed-bad) beaten-down reason and still correctly reject on valuation discipline alone -- the -58% drawdown is statistically dramatic but not statistically *cheap enough* relative to the real, unresolved regulatory/Senate-investigation risk still on the table. **Max Consultation Rule note:** no candidate reached Tier 1.5/Fast-track/Full Pipeline (died at Filter D within the direct scout, no B/C/D/E computation error -- this is a valid, non-fast-track outcome per CLAUDE.md § Scout Pipeline), so the mandatory Charlie pre-trade consultation was never triggered -- no BUY/SELL/TRIM action proposed, nothing to simulate/log beyond this rationale entry. No Full Pipeline run, no `reports/FICO_2026-09-29.md` written, no trade executed, no `dashboard/portfolio.js`/`trade-log.md`/`positions.md` changes (the existing stale `dashboard/portfolio.js` FICO watchlist row from 2026-05-19 was left untouched per Dashboard Write Safety targeted-patch-only scope -- this scout's fresh findings supersede it informationally via `portfolio/watchlist.md` but a formal dashboard sync was out of scope for a non-deploying single-ticker scout). **Methodology note for CIO/Charlie:** FICO's negative-book-equity (buyback-driven, -$2.10B) profile is the second live B-Zero case after BBWI confirming the leveraged-capital-return carve-out works as intended -- cleared through B-Zero without being treated as a distress signal. Counter-factual: N/A (no position taken, no candidate cleared). See `portfolio/watchlist.md` § FICO CIO-directed scout for full detail.

**Rationale (all 5):** Regime RISK-ON, Force Deploy ACTIVE, cash ~74% of NAV -- a full-pipeline-cleared candidate would have been live-eligible for a real BUY this round. This round targeted Electric Utilities + Independent Power Producers with AI-datacenter power-demand exposure, diversifying from a prior Telecom/Utilities round likely focused on regulated names, and deliberately checked the task brief's own hypothesis that IPP/merchant names (VST/CEG/NRG) might be riding near their highs on the AI-power narrative rather than beaten-down -- **that hypothesis was falsified honestly**: all three IPP/merchant-heavy names cleared Filter A decisively (-35% to -48%) on a well-documented, multi-source-confirmed September 2026 sector-wide selloff (surging Treasury yields >5%, oil >$100/barrel, Fed rate-hike jitters hitting rate-sensitive/growth-premium utility names broadly), while NEE (the most conventionally "regulated-utility" name) also cleared at -22.3 to -23.2% on Dominion-deal-uncertainty, and it was instead **AES** -- the most diversified/renewables-mix IPP -- that fell short of the 20% floor. All 4 Filter-A survivors were independently confirmed cyclical/sentiment (not structural impairment): each posted accelerating-or-reaffirmed underlying fundamentals (VST +31% EBITDA growth reaffirmed guidance despite an EPS miss; CEG raised guidance and added ~920MW of new PPAs despite a nuclear-AI-premium re-assessment; NRG reaffirmed and had earlier raised guidance despite a GAAP miss driven by acquisition-integration costs and a disclosed non-cash mark-to-market item; NEE's dislocation is pure M&A-deal-uncertainty on an otherwise-unchanged FPL/NextEra Energy Resources operating business). All 4 passed B-Zero clean on the negative-equity test, with NEE's and NRG's elevated Net Debt/EBITDA (7.36x and 7.16x respectively, both above the generic >4-5x threshold) judged differently per the task's explicit sector-leverage-context instruction -- NEE's judged a long-run structural utility-model constant (stable within its own 5Y range), NRG's judged a fresh, LS-Power-acquisition-driven step-up (a clear rising 3-period trend) -- both disclosed and carried forward to Filter E rather than uniformly treated the same despite superficially similar current ratios. Bucket classification found only CEG clearing the >15% YoY revenue-growth Growth-bucket trigger (+25.99%, hyperscaler-nuclear-PPA-driven, already visible in trailing revenue) -- VST's AI-datacenter-PPA thesis, while real and forward-looking, was honestly NOT force-fit into Growth given its trailing TTM revenue growth of only +3.80%, consistent with the task's explicit "check each honestly" instruction. **NEE, VST, and NRG all died at Filter B-Legacy (0/3 legs each), and CEG died at Filter B-V1 (0/3 OR-logic methods)** -- but this round produced the closest set of near-misses logged in the project to date: VST missed FCF yield by only 1.2 percentage points and EV/EBITDA by only 1.7% on 2 separate legs simultaneously, and CEG's closest B-V1 method (sector-average) missed by only 1.9%. NEE and NRG, by contrast, were decisive (not near-miss) rejects -- NEE's FCF yield is structurally negative every year in its 5-year history (a utility-capex-model feature, not a cyclical anomaly), and NRG's multiples, even after excluding two LS-Power-related distortion outliers per the CLAUDE.md contamination-exclude clause, still fail all 3 legs by a wide margin. **0/5 pass full A-E -- no Tier 1/1.5, no Full Pipeline, no trade this round.** VST and CEG both logged to `buy_list.md` as this round's near-misses, each with a specific, narrow re-check trigger (VST: ~2-5% further pullback or continued EBITDA growth could flip the EV/EBITDA leg; CEG: further pullback toward the sector-average benchmark, or a broader/cleaner peer set sourced with a fuller search budget). **Max Consultation Rule note:** no candidate reached Fast-track/Full Pipeline this round, so the mandatory Charlie pre-trade consultation was never triggered -- nothing to simulate/log beyond this rationale entry. No Full Pipeline run, no `reports/[TICKER]_2026-09-29.md` written for any of the 5, no trade executed, no `dashboard/portfolio.js` or `trade-log.md`/`positions.md` changes. **Sector-level flag for CIO/Charlie:** unlike Rounds 37-38/47 (fabless AI/semis and semicap equipment, where the entire sub-sector had structurally re-rated upward such that even large sentiment pullbacks failed to reach statistically-cheap multiples), this round's Utilities/IPP names show a genuinely different dynamic -- real, multi-source-confirmed cyclical dislocations that came *much* closer to clearing Filter B/B-V1 (VST within 1.2-1.7%, CEG within 1.9% on their closest legs), suggesting the sector's 2026 AI-power re-rating, while real (visible in CEG's own structural EV/Sales uptrend since FY22), has been more partially-priced/volatile than the fabless-chip/semicap-equipment sub-sectors' more one-directional re-rating. Worth a lightweight re-check on VST/CEG next round without a full 5-ticker re-scout, given how close both near-misses are. Counter-factual: N/A (no position taken). See `portfolio/watchlist.md` § Scout Report § Round 49 for full detail.

**Rationale (all 5):** Regime RISK-ON, Force Deploy ACTIVE, cash ~74% of NAV -- a full-pipeline-cleared candidate would have been live-eligible for a real BUY this round. This round targeted major US network/LCC airlines (DAL/UAL/LUV/AAL) plus one rail name (CSX) to diversify sub-sector exposure per task instruction, honestly checking each name's cyclicality rather than assuming a uniform sector outcome. **The entire group moved on the same Aug-Sept 2026 jet-fuel-spike shock** (WTI >$86-100 on Iran-conflict escalation, jet fuel >$4.71/gal near a 20-year high, all majors publicly discussing "marginal route" capacity cuts per Fortune's Sept 18 2026 reporting) -- but the depth of each name's drawdown diverged sharply based on hedging discipline and balance-sheet resilience: DAL/UAL (hedged and/or lower-leverage) fell shallowly (-10.6 to -18.9%, both short of the floor), while LUV (no fuel hedge since 2025) and AAL (heaviest debt load) fell hardest and both cleared Filter A. CSX, a rail name with a different cost structure, also fell short (-12.6%). **AAL's deep -28.5 to -30.2% drawdown made it look like the round's most attractive candidate on price alone, but it was correctly disqualified at Filter B-Zero** -- its negative equity (-$4.08B to -$6.73B) was rigorously distinguished from the BBWI/FICO precedent (both buyback-engineered, discounted and allowed to proceed) via Trefis's confirmation that AAL has been a *net share issuer* (5Y buyback ratio -1.00%), meaning the negative equity genuinely reflects accumulated operating losses and 2020-2021 COVID-recapitalization debt, not an engineered capital-return structure -- this is the project's first B-Zero case to land on the rule's "operating losses = reject at this gate" branch rather than the "leverage artifact = discount and proceed" branch, a useful calibration precedent for future airline/leveraged-legacy-industry scouts. **LUV, by contrast, cleared B-Zero cleanly as the round's strongest balance sheet** (sole investment-grade-rated major carrier, lowest leverage of any of the 5 tickers) and proceeded through Filter B (EV/EBITDA bottom-third leg) and Filter C (ROIC/WACC 79.4-84.3%, resolved via a clear 3-period improving trend consistent with the APTV precedent for borderline-but-improving candidates) -- but died at **Filter D** on a genuine methodology limitation: LUV's own through-cycle FCF is structurally negative/near-zero (the fuel shock plus heavy Boeing delivery-commitment capex), making the standard naive-FV (FCF÷WACC) proxy inapplicable rather than merely unfavorable, and the fallback analyst-PT-gap check (+19.34%) missed the 20% pass threshold by just 0.66 percentage points -- the closest near-miss logged this round. **0/5 pass full A-E -- no Tier 1/1.5, no Full Pipeline, no trade this round.** LUV logged to `buy_list.md` as the round's near-miss (Street-PT gap <1pp short); AAL logged as a B-Zero structural-reject case study, not a near-miss. **Max Consultation Rule note:** no candidate reached Fast-track/Full Pipeline this round, so the mandatory Charlie pre-trade consultation was never triggered -- nothing to simulate/log beyond this rationale entry. No Full Pipeline run, no `reports/[TICKER]_2026-09-30.md` written for any of the 5, no trade executed, no `dashboard/portfolio.js`/`trade-log.md`/`positions.md` changes. **Sector-level flag for CIO/Charlie:** this round demonstrates the task brief's own framing precisely -- airlines/rail are genuinely fuel-cost/demand-cycle-sensitive as a group, but within-sector dispersion driven by hedging discipline and balance-sheet resilience determines which specific names clear Filter A and, separately, which survive the capital-structure sanity check at B-Zero. The name with the single deepest, most "attractive-looking" price drawdown (AAL) was disqualified on balance-sheet grounds before valuation was even assessed, while a shallower-drawdown but clean-balance-sheet name (LUV) survived all the way to Filter D and missed by <1pp -- a useful illustration of why B-Zero's balance-sheet gate is sequenced ahead of the B/C/D valuation screens rather than folded into Filter E's overhang assessment alone. Counter-factual: N/A (no position taken, no candidate cleared). See `portfolio/watchlist.md` § Scout Report § Round 51 for full detail.

**Rationale (all 5):** Regime RISK-ON, Force Deploy ACTIVE, cash ~74% of NAV -- a full-pipeline-cleared candidate would have been live-eligible for a real BUY this round. This round targeted niche/specialty biotech distinct from the large-cap pharma already scouted (PFE/MRK/ABBV/BMY/GILD) and from REGN (already an OPEN position, itself a biotech, explicitly carved out per task instruction as context/comp only, not re-scouted) -- mRNA/vaccine platform (MRNA, BNTX), RNAi/rare-disease gene therapy (ALNY), and specialty rare-disease/immunology commercial-stage biotech (BMRN, INCY), diversifying sub-sector exposure within biotech per task instruction. **This round directly tested and substantially refuted the premise that this sub-sector is uniformly in post-COVID collapse** -- the task's own framing anticipated a "well-documented multi-year collapse" for MRNA/BNTX specifically, but the honest check found **3 of 5 names (MRNA, BMRN, INCY) trading near 52-week highs**, with MRNA having fully round-tripped to a fresh ATH on credible non-COVID oncology catalysts. Only BNTX and ALNY showed genuine beaten-down price action, and even within that pair the underlying stories diverged sharply on the critical cyclical-vs-permanent judgment call the task flagged by name: **BNTX's COVID-revenue collapse is real and structural (-93% quarter-over-quarter), but funded forward by a credible, well-capitalized Phase III-stage oncology pipeline** (not a bare one-product story) -- classified cyclical/sentiment with an explicit dissent, and it ultimately died on fundamentals (decisively negative ROIC) at Filter C regardless of the Filter A classification, which is the methodologically correct outcome per the task's explicit allowance that R&D-heavy clinical-stage ROIC can legitimately fail Filter C. **ALNY's collapse, by contrast, is a growth-deceleration-fear de-rating against genuinely improving fundamentals** (revenue, FCF, margins, and balance sheet all strengthening), a closer parallel to the project's PYPL (Round 52) precedent than to a CMCSA-style confirmed structural decline -- it is this round's one candidate worth a dedicated follow-up, routed to Tier 1.5 Learning-Queue specifically because two upstream filters (B-V1 peer sourcing, Filter C confidence) could not be rigorously completed within this round's explicit tight-budget instruction (max 2-3 web_search calls per ticker), not because the candidate itself showed a disqualifying signal. **0/5 pass full A-E, no Full Pipeline, no trade.** See `watchlist.md` § Scout Report § Round 54 for full detail.

All 3 classified cyclical/sentiment (not permanent impairment) and all 3 passed B-Zero clean-or-flagged-forward. All 3 were explicitly NOT force-fit to Growth despite double-digit headline revenue growth in ODFL/CHRW's case -- both diagnosed as pricing/yield-pass-through effects, not demand/volume growth. **All 3 then died cleanly at Filter B** -- a second data point (after Round 55's NXPI/MCHP) for a now-recurring pattern in this project: a genuine, confirmed cyclical price dislocation does not imply statistical cheapness when the same downturn compresses the trailing cash-flow/EBITDA denominator proportionally with (or faster than) the price. ODFL and JBHT both show EV/EBITDA at or above their own 5-year averages despite 20-30% price pullbacks. **0/5 pass full A-E -- no Tier 1/1.5, no Full Pipeline, no trade this round.** JBHT logged to buy_list.md as the round's furthest-through-funnel near-miss (closest FCF-yield read to the 6% bar). ## Scout Round 53 (Gold/Precious Metals Mining sector) -- 2026-10-01 (continued, historical entries above)

**Rationale (all 5, gold round):** Regime RISK-ON, Force Deploy ACTIVE, cash ~74% of NAV -- a full-pipeline-cleared candidate would have been live-eligible for a real BUY this round. This round targeted gold-mining operators (NEM, B, AEM) plus precious-metals streaming/royalty companies (WPM, FNV), diversifying from the earlier industrial-metals Materials round (FCX/NUE/LIN/MOS/CTVA) into a genuinely new sub-sector never before dedicated-scouted in this project. **Ticker note:** Barrick Gold Corporation renamed Barrick Mining Corporation and changed its NYSE ticker from GOLD to B effective May 6, 2025 (TSX ticker ABX unchanged) -- scouted here under its current ticker B. The task's own hypothesis (gold's 2026 bull run means most names are near highs, not beaten-down) was only half-confirmed: NEM, WPM, and FNV are all genuinely short of the 20% floor (-14.7%, -17.7 to -19.3%, -16.5 to -16.8%) despite a sharp Sept 30 2026 sector-wide gold-price-shock selloff (real US rates hit a multi-year high, gold broke below its 200-day MA, speculative-long unwind + pre-China-holiday de-risking), while B and AEM cleared Filter A decisively (-25.6%, -28.2 to -29.2%) -- the two largest-cap pure-play operating miners. Both Filter-A survivors were confirmed cyclical/sentiment (gold-price-cycle pullbacks, not structural impairment) -- B carrying additional resolved/resolving governance overhangs (CEO resignation, Mali mine seizure, Nevada Gold Mines JV dispute, all disclosed and judged non-disqualifying) and AEM showing a clean pure-commodity-price pullback with no company-specific red flag. Both passed B-Zero clean (B net-cash; AEM near-zero debt, the strongest balance sheet logged this round). **Bucket classification was this round's clearest test of the Growth-trigger-intent instruction**: both B (+31% TTM revenue growth) and AEM (+51.7%) show headline growth rates far above the mechanical 15% Growth-bucket trigger, but both were explicitly classified Value, NOT force-fit to Growth/B-V1, because the growth is 100% gold/copper-price-driven (not a deliberate reinvestment-phase demand story) and is accompanied by expanding FCF and active capital return (buybacks, dividend growth) -- the opposite signature of a Growth-bucket candidate. This is disclosed as the sector-level lesson of the round: commodity-price beta is not the same thing as organic growth, and applying the Growth trigger mechanically to a gold miner mid-bull-market would have been a scoring error. **B and AEM diverged sharply at Filter B**: B's FCF yield (7.33% on the primary NYSE-listing read; a TSX cross-listing read of 4.83% is disclosed as a near-miss on the same leg, not silently dropped) cleared the 6% bar, while AEM failed both legs computed (FCF yield ~5%, EV/EBITDA ~9.2x above its own 5Y median of 10.2x) -- consistent with Morningstar's own explicit framing of AEM shares as "expensive" even after the ~29% pullback, i.e. the market's premium for AEM's tier-one-jurisdiction quality persisted through the drawdown. **B proceeded to Filter C (PASS decisively, ROIC 23.74% ~=231% of a 10.26% WACC) and then Filter D, which is this round's central judgment-call test exactly per the task's own warning**: B's trailing FCF yield reflects earnings generated at the currently-elevated gold price (~$4,500-4,800/oz across multiple 2026 sources), not a through-cycle/mid-cycle level. Two independent methods -- the naive TTM-FCF-based proxy (implied FV $29.07, price trading at 1.40x that) and Morningstar's own through-cycle-adjusted fair-value estimate ($23.00, price trading at 1.77x that, Morningstar's own language: "89% premium") -- both agree the current price is priced for continued near-peak gold prices rather than a reversion to a mid-cycle level. A separate, far more bullish Street-PT read ($56.08 average, +37.9% implied upside) was explicitly checked and then discounted per CLAUDE.md's own rule that Street PT in commodity names often extrapolates the prevailing spot price rather than a mid-cycle normalization -- precisely the dynamic the task flagged by name ("use through-cycle gold price assumption, not current spot peak") -- rather than silently used to flip the Filter D outcome to a pass. **B dies decisively at Filter D, no Filter E pursued** (decisive fail, consistent with the task's tight-budget instruction not to force further computation past a clean reject). **0/5 pass full A-E -- no Tier 1/1.5, no Full Pipeline, no trade this round.** No near-miss meeting the project's typical sub-1-2pp-margin bar was logged to `buy_list.md` -- both Filter-A survivors died by wide (not marginal) margins at their respective terminal gates (AEM: a combined 2-leg Filter B miss, not a single-metric near-miss; B: a 1.40-1.77x valuation gap at Filter D, a decisive not marginal result). WPM is noted as this round's closest numeric Filter-A miss (-17.7 to -19.3%, within ~1-2.3pp of the floor) for audit-trail completeness, though it did not clear even the first gate. **Max Consultation Rule note:** no candidate reached Fast-track/Full Pipeline this round, so the mandatory Charlie pre-trade consultation was never triggered -- nothing to simulate/log beyond this rationale entry. No Full Pipeline run, no `reports/[TICKER]_2026-10-01.md` written for any of the 5, no trade executed, no `dashboard/portfolio.js`/`trade-log.md`/`positions.md` changes. **Sector-level flag for CIO/Charlie:** this round is a useful counter-example to a simplistic "gold bull market = all gold stocks near highs" assumption -- within-sector dispersion is wide (B/AEM cleared a 20%+ pullback while NEM/WPM/FNV did not), and the royalty/streaming sub-sector (WPM/FNV) shows a persistent structural valuation premium (FNV's independently-verified P/NAV ~3.15x, FCF yield only 1.79-2.9%) that did not fully unwind even in a sharp single-day, sector-wide selloff -- worth noting for any future royalty-sub-sector re-scout that the asset-light/zero-debt business model commands a premium multiple that is comparatively sticky. B's Filter D outcome is a clean illustration of the project's recurring commodity-cyclical lesson (also seen with FCX in the Materials round): trailing cash-flow-based valuation methods need an explicit through-cycle commodity-price adjustment, and a naive TTM-FCF read alone would have overstated how cheap the stock is mid-commodity-upcycle. Counter-factual: N/A (no position taken, no candidate cleared). See `portfolio/watchlist.md` § Scout Report § Round 53 for full detail.

The task's own framing was substantially confirmed: COST (quality compounder) sits only -16.04% off its own high, nowhere near a genuine dislocation, while TGT and ROST also show insufficient pullbacks despite real recent weakness. **KR and DG (2/5) cleared Filter A decisively.** DG received the task's explicitly-requested FICO/CMCSA-level rigor given its well-known multi-year value-destruction history -- the honest, evidence-based finding is that this specific crisis has credibly ended (5-6 consecutive quarters of hard, management-verified improving data), a materially different and more positive finding than a surface-level "DG = legacy value trap" assumption would produce, though the durability risk (tariff-refund non-repeat, shrink-gain deceleration) was disclosed not dismissed. **Both KR and DG then cleared B-Zero, bucket classification, Filter B, and Filter C cleanly** -- both are genuine, structurally sound cash-generative retailers with no balance-sheet red flags. **Both died at Filter D** -- a second consecutive sector where the valuation-gap discipline gate, not an earlier qualitative gate, proved the binding constraint (following the same final-gate pattern as Round 55's fabless-semis round, though via a different mechanism: here Filter B passed cleanly for both names, meaning the market has substantially already repriced both names' known issues rather than offering a residual overlooked discount). KR's Filter D miss (16.0% vs 20% bar) was materially closer than DG's (8.0-9.3% vs 20% bar), making KR this round's near-miss. **0/5 pass full A-E -- no Tier 1/1.5, no Full Pipeline, no Morgan QA, no Conviction Gate, no Max Consultation, no trade this round.** KR logged to buy_list.md as the round's near-miss.
